After she forgot an important medication refill for her sister and admitted to her primary care physician that she felt overwhelmed, mentally \"foggy,\" and unable to manage her usual detailed to, like my body and my mouth are both dragging behind my mind.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): It feels like my brain is full of cotton, so that even choosing what to cook for dinner or sorting my and those suspicious thoughts have been tangled together for much of this period.\n- depressive_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness_(Criterion C): Looking back over the past year or so, the deep sadness, lack of interest in my classes and groups, and feeling like there’s no point to anything have been there almost all the time, even during stretches when my thinking wasn’t quite as jumbled.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode_(Criterion B): There was a spell early on, before I started feeling this constantly low and empty, when overwhelmed, and not like myself has been going on since shortly after my sister’s heart problems started, which I’d say is getting close to a year and a half now.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: I first started having these fixed ideas that some of the hospital staff were working against us a little after my sister’s heart troubles began, and those suspicious thoughts have been hanging around It's more that I feel extremely slowed down and sluggish, almost like my body is moving underwater whenever I try to do simple things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your sleep patterns or your appetite since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've really lost my appetite over the past year—food doesn't appeal to me anymore, so I often just pick at my meals or forget to eat, and my clothes have gotten noticeably looser. As for my sleep, I haven't really noticed any major changes in my patterns.\n\nDoctor: During this difficult time, have you been struggling with feelings of worthlessness, or have you been blaming yourself excessively for things?"

It's more that I feel extremely slowed down and sluggish, almost like my body is moving underwater whenever I try to do simple things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your sleep patterns or your appetite since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've really lost my appetite over the past year—food doesn't appeal to me anymore, so I often just pick at my meals or forget to eat, and my clothes have gotten noticeably looser. As for my sleep, I haven't really noticed any major changes in my patterns.\n\nDoctor: During this difficult time, have you been struggling with feelings of worthlessness, or have you been blaming yourself excessively for things?\n\nPatient: Yes, I feel incredibly useless and overwhelmed, especially when I struggle to manage my sister's care. I constantly feel like I must have done something terribly wrong, which is why I'm convinced the hospital staff is quietly working against us and changing her charts.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a similar period of severe depression or a time when you felt this down in the past, or is this the very first time in your life you've felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall ever feeling this bad or having a period like this in the past. This is really the first time in my life I've felt so completely overwhelmed and down. It all feels very new to me."

It feels like a constant fog, and I just get completely stuck trying to do everyday tasks.\n\nDoctor: Along with this mental fog, have you been feeling persistently down, depressed, or lost interest in activities you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, most days I wake up with a heavy, sinking feeling in my chest that stays with me all day, making me feel like there's no point to anything. I constantly feel like I must have done something terribly wrong, which is why I'm convinced the hospital staff is quietly working against us and changing her charts.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a similar period of severe depression or a time when you felt this down in the past, or is this the very first time in your life you've felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall ever feeling this bad or having a period like this in the past. It was actually my older sister who developed serious heart problems about a year and a half ago, and taking on her care has been incredibly stressful.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this heavy, depressed mood and the difficulty concentrating?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on for a long time now, probably a little over a year. It really started sinking in shortly after I took over my sister's care, so it's getting close to a year and a half that I've been feeling this heavy and foggy.\n\nDoctor: Along with the mental fog, do you experience persistent physical exhaustion or bodily weakness even after doing very light physical tasks?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel physically exhausted or weak in that way. It's more that I feel extremely slowed down and sluggish, almost like my body is moving underwater whenever I try to do simple things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your sleep patterns or your appetite since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've really lost my appetite over the past year—food doesn't appeal to me anymore, so I often just pick at my meals or forget to eat, and my clothes have gotten noticeably looser. As for my sleep, I haven't really noticed any major changes in my patterns.\n\nDoctor: During this difficult time, have you been struggling with feelings of worthlessness, or have you been blaming yourself excessively for things?\n\nPatient: Yes, I feel incredibly useless and overwhelmed, especially when I struggle to manage my sister's care. I constantly feel like I must have done something terribly wrong, which is why I'm convinced the hospital staff is quietly working against us and changing her charts.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a similar period of severe depression or a time when you felt this down in the past, or is this the very first time in your life you've felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall ever feeling this bad or having a period like this in the past. This is really the first time in my life I've felt so completely overwhelmed and down. It all feels very new to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you recently heard any voices or seen things that other people cannot see or hear?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that at all. I don't hear any strange voices or see things that aren't really there.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned believing the hospital staff is changing your sister's charts. Do you feel like other people are also watching you, plotting against you, or trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like random people are watching me or trying to harm me in general. It's really just some of the doctors and nurses at my sister's hospital who I'm convinced are quietly working against us, changing her charts or delaying things because of something I must have done wrong."
